6.7.
Invariants of
T_{4,4}

Since the weight function depends only on the embedded topological type of the plane curve singularity, we can take
f=x^{4}+y^{4}=(x-y)(x+y)(x-iy)(x+iy)
; we then have
r=4
,
m=(1,1,1,1)
, and
c=(3,3,3,3)
. The Poincaré series are
P_{C_{i}}(t_{i})=1/(1-t_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq 4
,
P_{C_{\{i,j\}}}(t_{i},t_{j})=1
for
1\leq i<j\leq 4
,
P_{C_{\{i,j,k\}}}(t_{i},t_{j},t_{k})=1-t_{i}t_{j}t_{k}
for
1\leq i<j<k\leq 4
, and
P_{C}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},t_{4})=(1-t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4})^{2}
. Using these equations, or simply the fact that the union of any three branches is the
D_{4}
singularity of Subsection
6.5
together with standard results like (
2.2.4
), (
2.2.6
), and the symmetry (
2.3.2
), we find that the
w_{0}
-values in
R(0,c)
are as follows:

6.8.

T_{3,6}

1\leq i<j\leq 3

6.9.
Invariants of
T_{3,2b+3}
,
b\geq 2
.

Since the weighted lattice is an invariant of the embedded topological type, we can take
f=(x^{2}+y^{2b+1})(x+y^{2})
. Here
r=2
,
m=(2,1)
, and
c=(2b+4,4)
; the Poincaré series are
P_{C_{1}}(t_{1})=(1+{t_{1}}^{2b+1})/(1-{t_{1}}^{2})

P_{C}(t_{1},t_{2})=(1+{t_{1}}^{2}t_{2})(1+{t_{1}}^{2b+1}{t_{2}}^{2})

w_{0}

R(0,c)
and (the leftmost part of)
S_{-1}

6.10.
Invariants of
T_{2a+3,2b+3}
,
a,b\geq 1
.

By again using the invariance with respect to embedded topological type, we can take
f=(x^{2a+1}+y^{2})(x^{2}+y^{2b+1})
. Then
r=2
,
m=(2,2)
, and
c=(2a+4,2b+4)
, with Poincaré series
P_{C_{1}}(t_{1})=(1+{t_{1}}^{2a+1})/(1-{t_{1}}^{2})
,
P_{C_{2}}(t_{2})=(1+{t_{2}}^{2b+1})/(1-{t_{2}}^{2})
, and
P_{C}(t_{1},t_{2})=(1+{t_{1}}^{2a+1}{t_{2}}^{2})(1+{t_{1}}^{2}t_{2}^{2b+1})
. The complete
w_{0}
-table is similar to the previous case; we give the bottom-left corners of this and the space
S_{-1}
for sufficiently large
a,b
:

Note that here
\mathbb{H}_{1}(C,o)=\mathbb{Z}^{ab-2}
for
